                                                      423
8.9.6 Charging for Irreducible Right Shapes with Intersections

We consider the slack for irreducible right shapes with non-trivial intersections. Similar
to our intersection analysis for Q, for any level of intersection τ and γ that intersects
into τP , we factorize out the intersection component in γ = Y ◦ Ai ◦ Xi . . . At ◦ Xt for Y
a proper intersection-left-shape, and { Ai , Xi } the collection of rightwards rainbiw/MVS
extension pieces from the process of identifying rainbow MVS. This allows us to write
τP = τeP ◦ A1 ◦ X1 ◦ · · · ◦ At ◦ XT , and apply weight function and approximate coeﬃcient
for τeP .
    Formally, we deﬁne the base piece for γ as the following while the extension pieces are
identical to the kernel extension pieces for proper irreducible right shape.

Deﬁnition 8.9.22 (Base Piece for Kernel Intersection γ-shape). The base piece for intersection
of τ ◦ γ into τP is a shape Y obtained at the ﬁrst stage of applying rainbow MVS decomposition
process without applying any further rightwards extensions. In other words, it is deﬁned via the
following process,

    1. S(VY ) is a MVS separating UY ∪ Int(γ)τP and S(Vγ ) = S(VτP ) where Int(γ)τP is the set
        of vertices in V (γ) intersecting with vertices in τ beyond the boundary;

    2. Let E(γ − Y ) be the set of edges that can be reached from S(Vγ ) without passing through
        S(VY ), and deﬁne edges E(Y ) = E(γ) \ E( E(γ − Y );

    3. We additionally apply the TW update operation to give kernel partition on Y: UY = Vτ
        with inherited kernel partition, and T (VY ) is any vertex from T (Vγ ) \ S(VY ) that is either
        reachable from UY ∪ Int(γ)τP without using any edge in E(γ − Y ) or vertex not connected
        to UY ∪ Int(γ)τP ∪ S(Vγ ) but incident to edges.

    With the base piece deﬁned, observe it satisﬁes the following structural property by
our decomposition,

                                                  424
Deﬁnition 8.9.23. A shape Y is a base piece for kernel interesction if

      1. UY and VY both come with kernel partitions;

      2. S(VY ) is the MVS separating UY ∪ Int(Y );

      3. T (UY ) ⊆ T (VY ) are dormant in Y, and any vertex in T (VY ) \ T (UY ) is reachable from
         S(VY ) without passing through UY .

Recall that DY = T (UY ) ∩ T (VY ) is the set of dormant vertices in T, we deﬁne the approximate
coeﬃcient for Y as

8.10 Spectral Norm Bound for Graph Matrix for Color Shapes

We recall the deﬁnition of graph matrix for color-shape,

Deﬁnition 8.10.1 (Graph matrix for Color-Shape). Given a color-shape (α, CP), we deﬁne its
corresponding graph M(α)CP as a matrix with rows and columns indexed by any order-tuple of
[n] × [k] of size at most dsos
                           2 with entries given by



    M(α,CP) [(U, βU ), (V, β V )] :=            ∑                     ∑               ∏          χψ(i),ψ( j) ( G ) .
                                          ψ:V (α)→[n]          b (α)→[k]
                                                               β:V                 (i,j)∈ E(α)
                                            injective             b
                                                                  β∈CP
                                       U =ψ(Uα ),V =ψ(Vα ) b
                                                           β(U )= βU , βb(V )= β V


   Again, this is the usual graph matrix except each vertex receives an additional label in
[k] for its color, and CP is a grouping of coloring for vertices in the shape.


Trace moment method        Our proof proceeds by analyzing trace moments of matrices us-
                                                             1/2q
ing that for any A, the spectral norm ∥ A∥spec ≤ tr ( AA⊤ )q       . Taking q to be logarithmic
in the dimension of A suﬃces to get a bound on ∥ A∥ sharp up to 1 + δ for an arbitrarily
small δ > 0.
   The trace power can be expanded as a sum of weighted walks of length 2q over the
entries of Mα , and each walk can be viewed as a labeling of a graph composed of q blocks
                                                           
of α and α T : each term in the expansion of tr ( Mα Mα⊤ )q corresponds to a labeling of the
vertices in q copies of α and α⊤ with labels from [n] (i.e, vertices of the graph G) and a

                                                     430
label from [k ] satisfying some additional constraints that correspond to a valid walk. In
particular, we recover the usual graph matrix without colors if we restrict ourselves to
labels in [n]. The following deﬁnition captures these constraints.

Deﬁnition 8.10.2 (Color-Shape walk and its valid labelings). Let α be a shape and q ∈ N.
For each walk P, let GP (α, 2q) be the Color-Shape Walk graph of the shape-walk P vertices on
vertices VP (α, 2q) formed by the following process,

   1. Take q copies of α1 , . . . , αq of α, and q copies of α1T , . . . , αqT of α T ;

   2. For each copy of αi (and αiT ), we associate it with a labeling, i.e., an injective map ψi :
      V (α) → [n] (and respectively ψi′ for α T );

   3. For each i ∈ [q], we require the boundary labels to be consistent as ordered tuples, i.e.
      ψi (Uα ) = ψi′−1 (Vα ) and ψi′ (UαT ) = ψi (Vαi );

   4. Additionally, each such walk must be closed, i.e., ψq′ (VαT ) = ψ1 (Uα ) as a tuple-equality;

   5. Each vertex in VP (α, 2q) receive a color-label in [k ];

   6. We call each αi and αiT block a block-step in the walk.

For each walk-graph , we associate it with a natural decomposition P = ψ1 ◦ ψ1′ ◦ ψ2 ◦ · · · ◦ ψq ◦ ψq′ .

Proposition 8.10.3. For shape τ and any color-partition CP, for any q > Θ((|Uτ | + |Vτ |) poly log n),
for any ϵ > 0, we have

for some constant cnorm > 1.

       For application to our SoS lower bounds, standard to prior works in [BHK+ 16, GJJ+ 20b,
JPR+ 22, JPRX23, KPX24] we pick a global parameter q = Θ( DV ) so that the bound holds
for all graph matrices after a union bound over all shapes of concerned. We are now ready
to prove the block-value bound via race moment calculation.

                                                                 432
Proof. We apply trace moment method as introduced. The dependence of norm bound in
the factor of [n] follows straightforwardly from [JPR+ 22] and [KPX24] while we restate the
proof here for completeness and to highlight its adaptation to the factor of [k ], i.e., factors
incurred by identifying color of each vertex. Using ideas from Sec 2.1 in [KPX24] and
[HKPX23], the crux of ﬁnding Bq (τ, CP) is to identify a vertex/edge-factor assignment
scheme for each block-step in the trace expansion, and verify that

                             q
                         T
            E [Tr Mτ,CP Mτ,CP    ] ≤ Cost of Identifying the Start · Bq (τ, CP)2q .


for some candidate upper bound Bq (τ, CP) that upper bounds the contribution of each
step (or more formally, block-step). We refer interested reader to [KPX24] for a thorough
introduction, while we highlight the adaptation to our setting. In our case, the cost of
identifying the start is at most (nk )|Uτ | , and we note that we have (nk )|Uτ | ≤ (1 + ϵ)2q for
any ϵ > 0 provided q ≥ Θ(|Uτ | poly log n). That said, our focus is simply to identify a
candidate upper bound function Bq for each block, which would then give us

                                        q                          2q
                                    T
                       E [Tr Mτ,CP Mτ,CP    ] ≤ (1 + ϵ) · Bq (τ, CP)     .


Each block-function is associated with a factor-assignment scheme, and we consider the
following scheme.

   1. For each vertex appearing for the ﬁrst/last time in the given block, assign a factor of
      √
        n · (2q|V (τ ) \ Uτ ∩ Vτ |);

   2. For each vertex appearing for the middle time (but also not in Uτ ∩ Vτ ) in the given
      block, assign a factor of 2q|V (τ ) \ Uτ ∩ Vτ |;

   3. For each edge making a middle appearance (i.e. an H-step in the language of
                                   q
                                     1− p
      [KPX24]), assign a factor of    p .


                                               433
Claim 8.10.5. The above is a valid assignment scheme.

Proof. To see that this is a valid assignment scheme, note that one can write

                                       q
                                   T
                      E [Tr Mτ,CP Mτ,CP    ]=    ∑ ∏ E[χe (G)mulP (e) ]
                                                P:walk e∈ P


by straightforward trace expansion, and there are two kinds of factors in the above, vertex-
factor (for combinatorial counting) that is used to identify the walk, and analytical edge-
factor from expectation of the random variables.

   In our case, as we are working with normalized Fourier variables, observe that the
above is 0 if any edge appears only once, and each edge gives value 1 if appearing twice,
                        q       t −2
                           1− p
and moreover, a value       p         for any edge that appears t-times in the walk for t > 2.
                              q        t −2
                                  1− p
That said, we distribute the        p       factor of each edge to each block it makes a middle
appearance, and crucially, notice that we simply assign a factor 1 if the edge is making
ﬁrst/last time.

   For vertex factors (restricted to labels in [n]), notice to identify the walk, it suﬃces for
us to put down a label in [n] the ﬁrst time a vertex appears, and a cost of 2|V (τ )|q each
time the vertex appears beyond the ﬁrst time. Therefore, we crucially distribute the factor
                                                                              √
n to the ﬁrst and last time of which the vertex appears, which is therefore a n for each
appearance. Moreover, we give an additional factor 2|V (τ )|q for each time the vertex
appears as a loose upper bound.

   We next show how to relate the block-value bound Bq (τ ) to vertex separators. For each
block-step, include each vertex making middle appearance in the block into the set S.

Claim 8.10.6. S is a separator for τ unless some edge appears only once in the walk.

Proof. To see this, suppose S is not a separator, we have a path from Uτ to Vτ that does
not pas through S. By construction of our set S, no vertex along the path is making a

                                              434
middle appearance. Since by deﬁnition, a vertex cannot be making ﬁrst appearance in Uτ
or last appearance in Vτ , starting from the vertex making last appearance in Uτ , at some
point the path must pass through a switch-edge, an edge with one endpoint making last
appearance and one endpoint making its ﬁrst appearance. Any such edge appears only
once throughout the walk, and this proves the claim.

    That said, to upper bound the contribution of each BlockStep, it is suﬃcient for us to
upper bound its corresponding value from the set S as a separator. For each separator
S, notice the value of the blockstep is upper bounded by its corresponding factors on the
RHS. This completes the proof to our factors in n.
    To see the factor in k, consider the following factor-assignment scheme similar to the
above,
                                                                                           √
    1. For each color appearing for the ﬁrst/last time, assign a factor of                     k;

    2. For each color used by vertex outside Uτ ∩ Vτ appearing for the middle time, assign
        a factor of 2|V (τ )|q.

    3. Vertices in Uτ ∩ Vτ have their color ﬁxed, and therefore no factor is needed.

Therefore, for each block with coloring β for τ, its contribution of color-factor is at most
             | β(Uτ )|+| β(Vτ )|
k| β(τ )|−            2            · (2|V (τ )|q)|V (τ )\Uτ ∩Vτ | where we use a loose upper bound as only ver-
tices in V (τ ) \ Uτ ∩ Vτ may require encoding for its color. Since we do not optimize the
dependence of subpolynomial factors in this work, we subsume this factor into the depen-
dence of 2|V (τ )|q in our ﬁnal bound.
